# G0-G12 Owner Full Research Review - Corrective Synthesis - 2026-05-06

**Scope:** GTOS primitive-science program, G0 through G12 2026-05-06 artifacts
**Promotion verdict:** `NO_PROMOTION_VERDICT`
**Purpose:** owner-readable corrective synthesis and full row inventory after confusion over the phrase "only 2 passed"
**Generated from:** master mechanism, hypothesis, experiment preregistry, source contract registry, G12 decisions, G0 post-G12 closeout, and goal-status registry

## Owner Correction Mandate

This report exists because the previous closeout summary was easy to misread as: "G12 rejected all research except two ideas." That interpretation is wrong and must not be carried forward.

The owner objective was not immediate live promotion. The objective was deep research: generate mechanisms, hypotheses, preregistered tests, source maps, and then use existing data aggressively where possible. The red-team lane was not supposed to replace tests. A red-team blocker means "do not overclaim, do not mix labels, do not leak future data, do not mark source-safe, and do not promote." It does not mean "edge killed" unless an actual outcome test killed it.

This document therefore separates four states that were previously conflated:

1. **Registered research inventory** - useful ideas translated into rows but not tested yet.
2. **Existing-data discovery candidate** - should be tested now on local data, clearly labeled discovery.
3. **Blocked-before-outcome-test** - not because the idea is bad, but because the needed source/as-of/label/duplicate packet is not safe enough yet.
4. **Promotion/validation blocked** - cannot be called validated or live-ready, even if discovery results later look good.

## Direct Answer: Was The Narrowing Due To The Red Team?

Partly, but only at the governance/readiness layer.

G12 did **not** run backtests over the 77 mechanisms, 96 hypotheses, 97 preregs, or 86 sources. G12 reviewed leakage, duplicate counting, label separation, source validity, and CD2 proposals. Its job was to stop bad claims before they became validation language. It did not have evidence to say most ideas do not work in the market.

The number "2" came from CD2 reconciliation: out of 8 cross-domain second-pass assignments, only 2 produced machine-readable, schema-safe prereg rows that G0 accepted into the master preregistry. Those two were still outcome-closed and research-only. That was not a global ranking of all research utility.

## Was It Ridiculous To Kill Everything Without Tests?

Yes, if that is what had happened. It would be a bad research process to kill 77 mechanisms and 96 hypotheses without outcome tests.

Technically, that is not what the artifacts did. The artifacts registered the ideas and left them outcome-closed. But the closeout language was too defensive and too close to "rejected" language. The correct interpretation is: **not tested yet, not validation-safe yet, not promotion-safe yet.**

My honest opinion: the next action should not be another red-team pass. The next action should be an **Outcome Testing Governor** that opens a controlled research-only outcome-testing wave over the 97 preregs, starting with those compatible with existing local data and synthetic/path/lifecycle labels. Red team should then audit the test implementation, not decide market truth in advance.

## Why Have A Red Team At All?

A red team is still useful, but only in the right role.

Useful role:

- catch future-data leakage before a backtest accidentally cheats;
- catch duplicate counting where one setup creates many rows;
- catch label mixing between broker actual-R, synthetic path-R, no-fill lifecycle labels, and context labels;
- catch source validity errors such as Cboe/FRED/COT/BIS data joined by date when the data was not actually published yet;
- catch agent overclaiming and stop `NO_PROMOTION_VERDICT` drift.

Wrong role:

- decide a mechanism has no market edge without testing it;
- reduce the research program to only rows that are source-validation safe today;
- treat missing source contracts as evidence that an idea is bad;
- replace discovery backtests on existing data.

The red team knows methodology constraints. It does not know whether the market signal works unless a valid test was run.

## Experiment Testing Readiness - Initial Classification

This is my corrective next-action view. It is not a red-team rejection and not a final test result. It says what kind of testing lane each prereg appears to need.

| Testing class | Count | What it means |
|---|---:|---|
| `BROKER_ACTUAL_R_SEPARATE_OR_BLOCKED` | 10 | Keep broker actual-R separate. Run synthetic/path comparators if useful, but do not pool with broker actual-R until enough closed trades exist. |
| `CONTROL_OR_OBSERVATION_ONLY` | 33 | Do not R-backtest as an edge. Use it to improve data coverage, telemetry, source governance, candidate eligibility, or audit readiness. |
| `EXISTING_DATA_LIFECYCLE_OR_NO_FILL_CANDIDATE` | 10 | Run existing-data or current-shadow lifecycle/no-fill analysis now if row packets exist; otherwise add the missing capture fields first. |
| `EXISTING_DATA_SYNTHETIC_REPLAY_CANDIDATE` | 16 | Run existing-data synthetic replay/backtest now, label as discovery, report cost sensitivity and concentration. |
| `FORWARD_SHADOW_OR_PROSPECTIVE` | 7 | Start forward shadow capture or replay job. Do not pretend historical discovery is validation unless cohort was frozen before outcomes. |
| `SOURCE_ASOF_CLEANUP_BEFORE_OUTCOME_TEST` | 21 | First build parser/cache/as-of/no-lookahead proof. Then run discovery tests with publication-time-safe joins. |

## My Recommended Next Program

I would not accept the current state as a terminal research answer. I would treat it as the completed registry-building phase and start the testing phase immediately.

Recommended next goal: **G13 Existing-Data Outcome Testing Governor**.

Concrete sequence:

1. Build a machine-readable testing queue from `EXPERIMENT_PREREGISTRY_2026-05-06.json`.
2. Split rows into `existing_data_discovery`, `synthetic_path_replay`, `lifecycle_no_fill`, `broker_actual_r_waiting`, `source_asof_cleanup`, `forward_shadow`, and `control_only`.
3. For existing-data synthetic/path and lifecycle/no-fill rows, run tests now on local data. Report all as `DISCOVERY_ONLY_NOT_VALIDATION`.
4. For source-as-of cleanup rows, implement parser/cache/as-of tests first, then run outcome tests only on publication-time-safe joins.
5. For broker actual-R rows, do not block synthetic tests, but keep broker actual-R claims separate until enough closed account-history rows exist.
6. After tests, rank by expected system impact, robustness, sample size, concentration risk, and implementation path.
7. Only after that should G12 red-team the test outputs for leakage/duplicates/label errors.

My strongest opinion: the next wave should prioritize the rows that can improve the current system without waiting for perfect new data: path/replay, lifecycle/fill-no-fill, geometry/structure, V2/V2b/V3-adjacent no-retrace, execution friction, and local shadow-log diagnostics. Macro/vol/source-heavy rows should continue, but their first task is source-as-of cleanup, not outcome testing.

| `G10` | Execution risk | Pending lifecycle, fills/no-fills, slippage/costs, actual-R, path packets, exits | No source contract in G10 is validation_safe=true.; Broker actual-R evidence is too sparse for execution or exit promotion.; Close-side slippage/cost evidence is missing in current audit coverage.; Historical path replay lacks original POI bounds and broker lifecycle state. | Prioritize existing-data replay/path/structure testing and telemetry coverage. |
| `G11` | Data sources expansion | Source governance, data coverage, instrument expansion, source contracts | All G11 source contracts are validation_safe=false; No paid source access authorized or used; No outcome labels opened; G7/G8 completed lane outputs were not present at HEAD 42bf621c | Fix source/as-of contracts and convert proposal rows into machine-readable preregs. |
| `G12` | Red team | Methodology adversarial review, leakage, duplicates, label separation, source validity | No CD2 row is promotable after G12 review.; No source is validation-safe after G12 review.; No outcome review is opened after G12 review.; No survivor backlog row exists after G12 review. | Audit test outputs after tests, not before. |

Decay/fragility: Research velocity creates unlogged search degrees of freedom; promising variants become stale when future agents reuse old raw p-values without the trial ledger. |

## Appendix G - What To Do Next, In Plain Terms

1. Do not treat `survivor_backlog=0` as "nothing useful exists." Treat it as "nothing has gone through outcome testing plus validation/promotion gates yet."
2. Launch an outcome-testing wave. Start with preregs classified as `EXISTING_DATA_SYNTHETIC_REPLAY_CANDIDATE` and `EXISTING_DATA_LIFECYCLE_OR_NO_FILL_CANDIDATE`.
3. For every test, save the exact frozen cohort, data source paths, label family, duplicate policy, and metric before reading outcomes.
4. Report bad results too. Killing weak ideas with data is success; killing ideas by governance wording is not.
5. Keep G12 as an auditor of test validity. Do not let G12 become the market oracle.
6. After discovery tests, create a ranked shortlist by system impact: selection filters, better entries, better no-fill handling, exits/path management, risk/cost accounting, market awareness, data/source unlocks, and AI/ML improvements.

## NO_PROMOTION_VERDICT

This report does not promote live logic. It is a corrective synthesis and owner-readable inventory. It explicitly recommends research-only outcome testing on existing data where appropriate.
